# Rulings

**Status** · Every ruling, one line each (PROCESS-MECHANICS §9; P15): `K<n> · date · module or layer · the ruling · why`. A ruling is changed only by a new line naming its evidence and the one it replaces. Where a ruling has a fuller home, the line names it. Started by BOB #38, 2026-09-26, from the rulings made during the transition.

- K1 · 2026-09-25 · all · No jurisdiction in the product: local facts are data in jurisdiction profiles held by `jurisdictions`; outward text names CivicOS and the group · Bob's concern that Oakland references tell other groups CivicOS is not for them (`layers.md`, "No jurisdiction in the product")
- K2 · 2026-09-25 · all · No module size limit; size is a watched metric, reported near 4,000 lines · Bob (`layers.md`, rulings on the draft, 1)

- K156 · 2026-09-27 · process (P3, K113; civicos-process mechanics §2, §5, §6, §13, `roles/ROOT.md`) · The K154 revision: a ROOT session that Bob starts, at depth 0, is the parent of every BOB; a BOB that refreshes pushes its handoff and asks ROOT by one-time routine (`START BOB`) to start its successor, so every BOB sits at depth 1 and every job at depth 2, whatever the number of handovers. ROOT does nothing else and is replaced only by Bob (context past 300k tokens, or failed; the BOB's handoff then says `NEEDS BOB: start ROOT`). Every BOB reads its depth by following `parent_session_id` and writes it in the Status line; the 20-minute backstop reads each job's record as well as its session state, and a message pushed in a record is handled as if it had arrived. Routine-started sessions carry no connector, session or routine tools and are never ROOT, a BOB or a job. Certified by dry run D7, 2026-09-27 ~14:12 UTC (civicos-process `dryrun/CERTIFICATION.md` L1–L7, `RUNS.md` D7: ROOT started a sibling successor at depth 1, a job at depth 2 messaged its BOB by routine; 3.78M tokens) · Bob's design, 2026-09-27 (in BOB #47's session), replacing BOB #47's draft of a bounded chain (a BOB starting its successor only at depth 5 or less, Bob restarting it every seven BOBs); measured: the chain BOB #38 → #40 → … → #46 put BOB #46 at depth 7 and its jobs at 8.

- K168 · 2026-09-27 · process (K164; P3, P13, P16, P17) · Bob approves BOB #49's architectural simplification of the K164 revision: communication between sessions is **mailbox plus doorbell**. Each message is a numbered entry in its writer's own file in git (a job's record; BOB's outbox for that job on the tranche branch); each side keeps a cursor, the last entry it processed; a routine carries no content, only "check your mailbox", so a late, duplicated, reordered or lost routine cannot corrupt state, and BOB's backstop rings again for any side whose cursor is behind. Liveness is judged from git, never from platform status. A small tool, `tools/mail.mjs` in civicos-process, writes and reads the mailboxes so no session hand-writes protocol (R2's one failure). Kept from K164: ROOT, the WATCH dead-man (K167), the two-step close. Cut: the envelope grammar inside routines, the plan-line bookkeeping kept only for grading, and the 71-scenario suite as a standing requirement (P3 asks a small dry run). Certified by one small dry run; T7 then opens with its smallest layer first · Bob, 2026-09-27: "Simplicity is elegance."

And: a member closes a plan with a reason, a plan never closes itself. Adding the `action-plans` module he left to BOB. · P4, P5, P17.

- K613 · 2026-09-30 · the Action layer's notifications and filings (Bob, in BOB #74's session, answering K611's three points) · (1) Choosing an action includes setting its notification schedule, defaults preselected; a deadline notice's response offers another reminder or no further reminder (DEC-10, recorded in `NOTIFICATIONS.md` at D-125: remind again at a further increment, or stop; DEC-69: never nag). So the overdue/deadline reminder is not repeated unless the member asks, and the class question of K611 (1) is answered by the member-set schedule, not by re-notification. (2) A litigation-hold reminder, like every notification, is not repeated unless the member asks; the hold may be cleared when responding to it, and if not cleared it stays open until cleared: that is its disposing door. (3) Filing templates: every filing has case-specific parts; a group builds a library of boilerplate templates over time; where none fits, members use the assistant to draft one for the action at hand (a proposal a member adopts, D2), and a derivative of it may be kept as a template for later use. No job invents legal text. The drafts' citation of DEC-70 for notifications is a mis-citation (DEC-70 is basis-leg severance); ACTION_DESIGN #1 corrects it and folds these three answers into its drafts. · P5, P17.
- K614 · 2026-09-30 · the Action layer's reminders (replaces K613 (1)'s citations; evidence: DEC-94, Bob 2026-09-29, in `docs/development/DECISIONS.md` on `claude/gallant-brown-zg0wc1`, PR #4, not yet on `main`) · The governing ruling is DEC-94: (1) a deadline reminder is the member's own request, set up when the action is chosen in the action plan, perhaps by a default the member sees and can change at that moment (nothing preselected unseen, DEC-77); a further reminder is one the member accepts, perhaps at the system's suggestion; it fires as asked; (2) as a deadline nears, the item's position, colour or wording may change: display, not a notification; (3) no outside channel (email, push); (4) overdue is new and re-notifies once (DEC-10); "due within N days" is not new unless the member asked for it. K613 (2) (the litigation hold stays open until cleared, cleared when responding) and (3) (the template library) stand. The Action drafts cite DEC-94 (not DEC-70) and fold its four points; PR #4's DEC-86–DEC-95 reach `main` with T17's close. · P5, P15, P17.

- K1254 · 2026-10-02 · DEC-112 (5) (K1134 Q6), Bob's doctrine · **Bob: anonymity cannot carry a case.** "The system can't allow anonymity to be used for abuse. Imagine a corrupt group. An anonymous member(s) could falsely attest to 'evidence' … the published case would rest materially on anonymous and corrupt sources. That can't happen. Anonymous sources can lead investigators to other sources, but they can't be the case of a CivicOS case, because CivicOS is based solely on evidence - not narrative." Read as: material whose only attestation is an off-the-record source is a lead, never a basis; no load-bearing member may rest on it, and `publishCase` refuses a case where one does. This amends DEC-112 (5) ("their material travels whole with its attestations … their grades recreate like any other"); K1134 Q6 (the project's and the group's attestations) is moot and withdrawn. NOTICE to UX-DESIGN to amend DEC-112 (§13.1 (5)). Asked of Bob, each with BOB's recommendation: (a) does off-the-record material appear in the published case at all (recommend: no, it stays in the project as a lead); (b) does the rule reach a named member's own uncorroborated capture, which DEC-81 lets carry a case with a stated reason (recommend: no, a named member and the signing group are accountable; the rule is about anonymity). N519 in `next.md`. · P5, P17, §9, §13.1

- K1431 · 2026-10-05 · requirements, Bob's · Bob, 2026-10-05: many things must be done by a date for reasons other than a regulation: a budget must be in place before its period begins, a report must be available before the meeting that decides on it, and so on. So a due date's basis may be a law or order, a commitment, a dependency (the event it must precede, and why), or the group's own window; a dependency date is derived from the date of the event it serves and never presented as a legal deadline, and missing it is a dated fact about sequence ("the report was published after the vote"), never a violation. The layer-9 contract's "every deadline names the statute, order or commitment it comes from" reads "names its basis" in this sense when it is next amended. - K1440 · 2026-10-05 · requirements (B4) · One held obligation object (§3.1), home of the flow model. A duty computed from a profile rule or proposed by the machine stays a proposal until a member adopts it in one act; from then it is tracked and told once, whatever its basis kind (K1431). - K1442 · 2026-10-05 · doctrine reading (B6) · Dated, graded, cited lines may be walked: bounded, as of a date, the weakest hop governing, undetermined on exhaustion, with no centrality or ranking; constitutive relations stay untraversed (entities R26). - K1443 · 2026-10-05 · doctrine reading (B7) · The machine writes, attributed to itself and shown for review, only: lines and holders from source-native data with identifiers at both ends (Legistar seats); profile offices seeded at setup; a proceeding registered from a captured register or caption; reader-stated dates with their method. - K1444 · 2026-10-05 · requirements (B9) · (i) Direction-aware uncertain dates: the group's own deadlines use the earliest candidate date; a body is overdue only after the latest, and "possibly overdue: undetermined, because …" between (K941 is read as the group's side of this). (ii) progressions R16: a body's overdue duty runs from the event's own date, never the capture date; without one it is undetermined with the capture date shown as a bound. (iii) actions R12's governing day is the local day of the office's or venue's jurisdiction, not the UTC day, and a rule may say "by close of business" in the office's hours. Why: each corrects a claim the record makes about a body today (D77 ranks overclaiming worst; a typed clock reads overdue 7–8 hours early); this is the correctness of the time engine, which BOB is better placed to judge than Bob. · P5, P7, P17 (K1437).

- K1452 · 2026-10-05 · requirements, doctrine, Bob's (B8) · Bob rules B8, setting aside all three options: "Tracking the activities, statements, and connections between individuals is - and has always been - an essential element of investigations." People are a construct the system fully supports: who holds a post and their name; the other jobs they have held, at the city or elsewhere; their schools, degrees, licences, military service and other credentials; an organisation's departments, staffing and people, today and in the past; a person's history as the substrate for who knows who, even when their current circumstances do not connect them; and organisations of every kind (government agencies, companies, nonprofits, social clubs, associations, political groups). This replaces the doctrine row "Offices, never private individuals" of the capability ladders (§10) and synthesis §5 B8 (a). - K1471 · 2026-10-05 · doctrine, Bob's (rankings) · Bob: "there are facts and there's the analysis that results from ... analyzing factual data (and other previously generated analysis). A machine can rank the biggest expenses in the 2013 budget, the contractor who has gotten the most business from the department of public works, the city council member that's been in office the longest, and so on. And the analysis module can generate analysis that's added to the record (and kept up to date), which might include fact-based rankings." A ranking by a stated, measured quantity is analysis and is allowed: a calculation that names its quantity, scope and period, cites its inputs, records its method, is stored and recomputed when its inputs change, and states what it could not count. What stays forbidden is a score standing for a judgment: importance, suspicion, significance or severity (DEC-89; conformance R8), one composed score across measures (DEC-82, DEC-92), and network measures such as centrality or "most connected" across mixed kinds of link (practice survey; D53, D91). A count of one kind of link is a fact ("held 7 board seats"). BOB's reading of the existing rules: `query-language` R11 (relevance order published, the score never, for sight) and conformance R8 are unchanged. The second study's C2 rows 2 and 13, C3 and C8 and the ladders' doctrine row are worded accordingly. · P5, P17.

- K1473 · 2026-10-05 · doctrine, Bob's (machine signals; second study C12) · Bob: "I agree with your recommendation" (C12 (a)), after asking why the machine may not fold factual measures into one score, or suggest from facts the importance, suspicion or significance of something, or that an earlier event, decision or appointment was a factor in later ones: "It's important not to judge facts or analysis, but it's equally important that they not be ignored or devalued as signals on which hunches and hypotheses might be founded." Ruled: a combined measure with a stated formula is a computed fact (analysis, K1471); a score standing for a judgment (importance, suspicion, significance, a possible cause) is a signal, which the machine may produce only with its method, inputs, each input's contribution and its measured false-alarm rate, shown only after a false-alarm test; a member may take it up as a hunch or hypothesis (K1467); it is never cited by a claim, never moves a grade or a finding, and is never published unless a member proves the point with evidence; a suggested cause stays "proposed: may have been a factor" until evidence makes it a Cause (DEC-84 (10)); a signal attaches to a pattern or a transaction, and suspicion is never stored as a label on a person. This amends DEC-22 (the machine may raise signals; members still choose what to look into), DEC-82 and DEC-92 (one composed score allowed as a signal or a declared formula, never as a grade), and DEC-89 (significance only as a signal). Folded: capability ladders §10 (new row) and every "no score standing for a judgment" passage; the second study's C2 rows 2 and 13 and C8 reworded. Channel read first (§13.1 (3c)). · P5, P17.
